Search Analysis & Statistics Parallel Academic Features

Parallel Academic Features: English-Mirroring Usages in Contemporary vs. Pre-Modern Arabic

The author’s table (per category) documents contemporary Arabic academic expressions that mirror English scholarly usage, with attestation based on Al-Maktaba al-Shamela, SHD, and DHD. The collapsible supplementary table below each category — generated by Claude — presents confirmed phraseological calques in four columns: (1) modern Arabic calque and collocations, (2) English corpus attestation with phrase bold-highlighted, (3) corresponding pre-modern Arabic sentences with classical equivalent bold-highlighted, (4) commentary. Only calques directly mirroring English structure and absent from pre-modern classical Arabic are included. This supplementary section does not form part of the author’s appendix.

Research-Related Terminology
#Arabic item & descriptionEnglish in corpusPre-modern Arabic equivalentsAttestationLive corpus matches
1.Adabiyyāt : (literature), exemplified by murāja ʿ at al-adabiyyāt (literature review). A search for this exact phrase yields over 1,000 hits in Google Scholar, approximately 900 in Al-Mandumah, and nearly 7,000 in the Saudi Digital Library (SDL). This is limited to the exact expression; other related collocations (e.g. madhkūr fī al-adabiyyāt /mentioned in the literature, c. 100 hits in SDL) would further increase overall frequency. The isolated term adabiyyāt was not searched due to its polysemy and the need for manual disambiguation.all the literature on that/There exists a literature dealing with it/mentioned in literatureوجميع ما كتب في ذلك/وضعت فيها التّآليف / ذكره المصنّفون
Unattested in a cursory Shamela search; the SHD gives its earliest attestation as 1933.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
2.Muqāraba (approach), in the sense of a method or framework used to address an issue. A search in Al-Mandumah for its plural form, muqārabāt , yields 5,647 hits. The plural was searched in preference to the singular due to the latter's polysemy with the sense of physical or metaphorical 'approaching' or 'coming close', whereas the plural is used predominantly in the methodological sense.However, some students have occupied themselves with the (mixed) approach /that is one approach ,” he said, “but how did you select a different approach /Their approaches to the discipline differed in (the degree of emphasis they placed upon) verification and argumentation.إلّا أنّ هذه الطّريقة قد يعنى بها بعض طلبة العلم/فقال: هذا مذهب ، فكيف اخترتَ غيره؟/واختلفت طرائقهما في الفنّ بين التّحقيق والحجاج
Unattested in a cursory Shamela search; the DHD gives its earliest attestation as 1983.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
3.Munāqasha (discussion), in the sense of discussing, considering, or commenting on an issue. In pre-modern Arabic, however, the term appears to have been semantically narrower, referring more specifically to critical scrutiny, disputation, or rigorous examination. Notably, searches for the term in the Arabic–English corpus frequently yield translations such as 'closely examine' and 'dispute'. A cursory search for munāqasha in Al-Mandumah and SDL shows extensive contemporary usage across various collocations: for example, tammat munāqashat ... (“it was/has been discussed”) yields around 650 hits, munāqasha ʿ āmma (“general discussion”) around 900, and al-munāqasha al-sābiqa (“the previous discussion”) around 300 in SDL. The isolated term munāqasha itself yields over 130,000 hits, though these would require manual filtering to distinguish the broader English-mirroring contemporary sense of “discussion” from the older sense of disputation or critical scrutiny.In the previous discussion , we have always stated/ This should be understood and be kept in mind in the following discussion / in this book he discusses fevers.قد تقدّم لنا في جميع ما مرّ أنّ/ فافهم ذلك واعتبره فيما نورده عليك من بعد/ تكلم في هذا الكتاب عن الحميات
Collocations such as munāqasha ʿ āmma , tammat munāqasha , and munāqasha sābiqa are unattested in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela. The term munāqasha itself appears mainly in its older sense of disputation and critical examination.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
4.Ḥ aql (field), mirroring English 'field' in the sense of an area of study or interest, extended metaphorically from the idea of cultivated land. Searches in databases yield thousands of occurrences; for example, ḥ aql al-dalāla (semantic field) exceeds 1,400 hits in SDL, ḥ aql al-tārīkh (field of history) around 500, and ḥ aql al-ba ḥ th (research field) over 500, among many others.scholar in this field /On what fields of knowledge a physician should know/the science of the religious law came to consist of two kinds. One is the special field of jurists and muftis.صاحب هذا الفن /ما يجب على الطبيب معرفته من العلوم /وصار علم الشّريعة على صنفين: صنف مخصوص بالفقهاء وأهل الفتيا
Cursory searches suggest that the isolated term is unattested in this sense, while the collocations likewise appear unattested in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela. The SHD records its earliest cited attestation in the late twentieth century.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
5.Mushkila , as in mushkilat al-ba ḥ th/mushkila ba ḥ thiyya, mirroring English 'research problem'. The term appeara highly standardised in contemporary academic Arabic; for example, the Arabic expressions mushkilat al-ba ḥ th/mushkila ba ḥ thiyya yields over 74,000 results in SDL.He sets aside a special chapter for a particular problem /In the field under consideration here, we encounter (certain) problems, treated incidentally by scholars/Or the invention of problems and topics of research which occur to a competent scholarإنّما يبوّب الباب للمسألة /وهذا الفنّ الّذي لاح لنا النّظر فيه نجد منه مسائل تجري بالعرض لأهل العلوم/استنباط مسائل ومباحث تعرض للعالم
Cursory searches suggest that mushkila (problem) in isolation is used in the sense of an obscure or problematic issue, rather than in the English-mirroring sense of an open interrogative issue requiring inquiry and investigation. In addition, collocations such as mushkilat al-ba ḥ th/mushkila ba ḥ thiyya (research problem), al-mushkila al-ra ʾ īsiyya (the main problem), yu ʿ āliju mushkila (treats/addresses a problem), and yunāqishu mushkila (discusses a problem) yield no results in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela .
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
6.Na ẓ ariyya , mirroring English theory . The term appears highly standardised in contemporary academic Arabic; only in its plural form na ẓ ariyāt (theories), it yields over 100,000 results in SDL.This is an unlikely theory / In Sufi discussion, there appeared the theory of the poleوهو قول بعيد / وظهر في كلام المتصوّفة القول بالقطب
Cursory searches in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela suggest that na ẓ ariyya (theory) in this sense is unattested. Both the Doha Historical Dictionary (DHD) and the Sharjah Historical Dictionary (SHD) indicate it as a modern usage, with earliest recorded attestations dated to 1852 and 1911 respectively.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
7.Sāhama/ musāhama , corresponding directly to English contribute and contribution in academic usage. The terms are highly frequent in contemporary scholarly Arabic, yielding thousands of hits in SDL; for example, musāhama kabīra (great/major contribution) exceeds 2,000 occurrences, musāhama ʿ ilmiyya (scholarly contribution) over 900, and musāhama ba ḥ thiyya (research contribution) over 550.Ibn Sidah's own contribution was an attempt to give the etymologies/he contributed greatly by his labors to the general stock of information/ contributed to [the information] of othersوزاد فيه التّعرض لاشتقاقات.../ وانتفع الناس به/ أفاد
Cursory searches in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela suggest that s āhama/musāhama (theory) in this sense is unattested.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
8.Fajwa , directly corresponding to English gap in the sense of an absence, deficiency, or missing element requiring further completion or investigation. The term appears highly standardised in contemporary academic Arabic and yields many thousands of hits in SDL; for example, fajwa ba ḥ thiyya (research gap) alone yields over 4,000 results.As a result, I was able to fill the gaps in my historical informationفـزدت ما نقص من أخبار
Cursory searches in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela suggest that fajwa (gap) in this sense is unattested.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
9.marji ʿ (reference) and a ʿ mal/kitāb marji ʿ ī (reference work/book), directly corresponding to English reference and reference work/book. These forms are highly common in contemporary academic Arabic; in SDL, qā ʾ imat al-marāji ʿ (list of references) yields over 20,000 hits, while kitāb marji ʿ ī (reference book) yields over 600 and ʿ amal marji ʿ ī (reference work) over 350.is book, thus, became the basic reference work /AlKhunaji's books are used by contemporary easterners as reference works / the Kitab at-taysir became the general reference workفصار إماما للمؤرّخين يرجعون إليه /وعلى كتبه معتمد المشارقة لهذا العهد/ واعتمدوا كتاب التّيسير
Cursory searches in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela suggest that marji ʿ (reference) in this sense is unattested. The Sharjah Historical Dictionary records its earliest attestation as the mid-twentieth century.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
10.Maṣdar , directly corresponding to English 'source' in the sense of a person or entity from which information is obtained. The term is highly standardised in contemporary academic Arabic and yields many thousands of hits in SDL; for example, maṣādir awwaliyya (primary sources) alone yields over 6,000 results .their principal source for verifying historical information/ I referred to the source whence I derived the Tradition and found the word written as he said/ I never ceased consulting historical works and other sources where that information might be expected to be foundوأصلا يعوّلون في تحقيق الكثير من أخبارهم عليه /رجعنا إلى الأصل فوجدناه كما قال/ وما زلت أتطلب تاريخ وفاة ابن هانيء المذكور من التواريخ والمظان التي يطلب منها فلا أجده
Cursory searches in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela suggest that maṣdar (source) in this sense is unattested. The Doha Historical Dictionary classifies it as modern, with earliest attestations in the second half of the nineteenth century. By contrast, the Sharjah Historical Dictionary appears to date its usage back to the thirteenth century, citing an example such as laysa fī al-maṣādir … (“[this word] is not in the maṣādir [inteperted as sources]…”), although this exact phrase is also frequently attested in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela in the traditional grammatical sense of maṣdar as a verbal noun, rather than in the modern sense of 'source'.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
11.Dirāsa , directly corresponding to English 'study' in the sense of a scholarly investigation, research work, or academic field, rather than merely the general act of learning or studying. The term is highly frequent in contemporary academic Arabic and yields thousands of results in SDL across numerous collocations; for example, dirāsat al- ḥ āla (case study) yields over 145,000 hits, dirāsāt islāmiyya (Islamic studies) over 45,000 hits, tunāqishu al-dirāsa (the study discusses) over 1,700, tasta ḥ iqqu al-dirāsa (deserves study) over 1,000, and dirāsa jādda (serious study) over 900.deserve study /He went beyond (Ibn al-Khatib's) studies and investigations/ make a thorough study of their legal statusيصلح أن يبحث /فأوفى على أنظاره وبحوثه / لتحقيق أحكامها الشّرعيّة
Cursory searches in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela suggest that dirāsa (study) in this sense is unattested. Searches for many of its contemporary academic collocations likewise appear unattested.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
24.qarār (decision), mirroring several English phraseological patterns involving 'decision'. Numerous such usages are highly frequent in contemporary academic Arabic writing; for example, in SDL ṣun ʿ al-qarār (decision-making) yields over 19,000 hits, qarār siyāsī (political decision) over 7,500, and yattakhidh qarār (to take a decision) over 3,000.They do not have executive authority to make decisions in (these councils)/The man who leads the prayers and makes decisions in religious matters is called priest/ They said, 'We submit to the decision of Sa'd ibn Mu'adh.ولم يكن لهم فيها من الحلّ والعقد شيء/ ويسمّون الإمام الّذي يقيم الصّلوات ويفتيهم في الدّين بالقسّيس/ فَقَالُوا نَنْزِلُ عَلَى حُكْمِ سَعْدِ بْنِ مُعَاذٍ،
Cursory searches in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela suggest that such English-mirroring phraseological patterns involving qarār are unattested.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches
25.ijrā ʾ (procedure), mirroring several English phraseological patterns involving 'procedure/measure'. Numerous such usages are highly frequent in contemporary academic Arabic writing; for example, in SDL ijrā ʾ ta ḥ līlī (analytical procedure) yields around 17,000 hits, al-ijrā ʾ al-mutba ʿ (the procedure followed) around 4,000, al-ijrā ʾ āt al-ṣa ḥ ī ḥ a (correct procedures) over 400, and al-ijrā ʾ al- ṭ ibbī (medical procedure) over 500.Thus will the water be very caustic indeed. This is used for many medical procedures / The correct procedure to be observed before and after cupping by him who is to be cupped or venesected/ The right procedure is well known to him who has experience in this art.فإنه يكون حينئذ قوي الحدة جداً، ينصرف في كثير من أعمال الطب /ما ينبغي أن يدبر به المحتجم والمفتصد قبل الحجامة/ وليس يخفى طريق الصواب على من كانت له دربة بهذه الصناعة إن شاء الله
Cursory searches in Al-Maktaba al-Shamela suggest that such English-mirroring phraseological patterns involving ijrā ʾ are unattested. The Doha Historical Dictionary classifies the relevant usage as modern, with attestations dated to the twentieth century.
Not clearly attested in cursory searches

Phraseological calques from English into modern academic Arabic, identified by scanning all 1,307 parallel pairs across 12 pre-modern texts. Only calques confirmed to directly mirror English structure and absent from pre-modern classical Arabic are included. Col 1: modern Arabic calque & collocations. Col 2: each numbered example shows the English corpus sentence (calque phrase in amber) paired directly with the corresponding pre-modern Arabic source sentence (classical equivalent in green) — showing what classical Arabic used instead of the modern calque. Col 3: commentary. This table does not form part of the author’s appendix.

← role

Definition plays a major role in teaching wisdom, because a concise statement such as “a mortal speaking being” denotes a single individual of the Homo sapiens species, which is countless and limitless in number.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb

while it has the role of a genus in relation to the species beneath. It is a similar case with the term “species”. It can either have no other species beneath it and comprise only individual things, in which case it is then known as “the species of species”.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb

3
The “nourisher” uses the food in a way that gives nourishment to the body, without wastage. These three faculties can either have the role of being masters only, such as the “procreator”, or they can have the joint role of servant and master.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
والغاذية: هى التي تغذو البدن، فيخلف عليه بدل ما يتحلل منه. وهذه القوى الثلاث بعضها مخدومة فقط، كالمولدة، وبعضها خادم ومخدوم.
4
When Tamim received this letter, he had doubts whether the name was Khunais or Hubaish, and gave orders to see if it was inscribed on the role of the army. Six names were found; some of them Khunais and the others Hubaish; so, he sent all those men to the poet.1282CE.wafayat-ibn-khallikan
فلما ورد الكتاب على تميم تشكك في الاسم فلم يعرف أخنيس أم حبيش. ثم قال: انظروا من له مثل هذا الاسم في عسكرنا، فأصيب ستة ما بين خنيس وحبيش، فوجه بهم إليه.
5
In their opinion, the prophetical soul has special essential qualities which produce wonders, with the help of the power of (the Necessary per se) and the obedience of the elements to Him for purposes of generation. (The role of) the prophet (in this process), in their opinion, is that through those qualities that God put into him, he is by nature fitted for being active among (all) created things, whenever he addresses himself to them and concentrates on them.muqaddimah
وإنّ النّفس النّبويّة عندهم لها خواصّ ذاتيّة منها صدور هذه الخوارق بقدرته وطاعة العناصر له في التّكوين والنبيّ عندهم مجبول على التّصريف في الأكوان مهما توجّه إليها واستجمع لها بما جعل الله له من ذلك
6
Subsequently, as at the present time, the matter has become most important in connection with harmony in (military) protection and the administration of public interests. Group feeling has come to play a role in it. (Group feeling is) the secret divine (factor that) restrains people from splitting up and abandoning each other. It is the source of unity and agreement, and the guarantor of the intentions and laws of Islam. When this is understood, God's wise plans with regard to His creation and His creatures will become clear.muqaddimah
ثمّ صارت اليوم من أهمّ الأمور للإلفة على الحماية والقيام بالمصالح فاعتبرت فيها العصبيّة الّتي هي سرّ الوازع عن الفرقة والتّخاذل ومنشأ الاجتماع والتّوافق الكفيل بمقاصد الشّريعة وأحكامها.
7
In the Book on Politics ascribed to Aristotle, Aristotle mentioned that its real significance is to frighten the enemy in war. Frightful sounds do have the psychological effect of causing terror. Indeed, as everyone knows from his own (experience), this is an emotional element that plays a role on battlefields. The explanation given by Aristotle - if it was he who gave it - is correct in some respects.muqaddimah
وقد ذكر أرسطو في الكتاب المنسوب إليه في السّياسة أنّ السّرّ في ذلك إرهاب العدوّ في الحرب فإنّ الأصوات الهائلة لها تأثير في النّفوس بالرّوعة ولعمري إنّه أمر وجدانيّ في مواطن الحرب يجده كلّ أحد من نفسه وهذا السّبب الّذي ذكره أرسطو إن كان ذكره فهو صحيح ببعض الاعتبارات.
8
People who work with talismans, therefore, need (only) a little exercise to give the soul the power to bring down the spirituality of the spheres. Devotions and exercises certainly play a very insignificant role in it. The opposite is the case with people who work with words. Their exercise is the most extensive that can be. It is not for the purpose of being active in the existing things, since that is a veil (standing between them and their real task). Such activity comes to them accidentally, as an act of divine grace.muqaddimah
ويحتاج أهل الطّلسمات إلى قليل من الرّياضة تفيد النفس قوّة على استنزال روحانيّة الأفلاك. وأهون بها وجهة ورياضة. بخلاف أهل الأسماء فإنّ رياضتهم هي الرياضة الكبرى، وليست لقصد التصرّف في الأكوان إذ هو حجاب. وإنّما التصرّف حاصل لهم بالعرض، كرامة من كرامات الله لهم.
9
He possessed an unequaled linguistic habit. His pupils followed in his footsteps. In general, the (Arabic linguistic) habit plays a greater role in Spain, and instruction in it is simpler and easier (there than elsewhere), because the (Spaniards) are nowadays greatly interested in, and concerned with, philology and literature and the teaching tradition in those (subjects), as we have mentioned before.muqaddimah
وكان له في اللّسان ملكة لا تدرك واتّبع أثره تلميذه من بعده. وبالجملة فشأن هذه الملكة بالأندلس أكثر وتعليمها أيسر وأسهل بما هم عليه لهذا العهد كما قدّمناه من معاناة علوم اللّسان ومحافظتهم عليها وعلى علوم الأدب وسند تعليمها.
يلعب دوراً calques the theatrical English metaphor "play a role". Pre-modern Arabic expressed functional agency through يقوم مقام (stands in the place of), كان شأنه عظيماً (its matter was great), or له أثر (it has an effect). The collocational frame دور + adjective mirrors English "[adj.] role" systematically.
علاوة على ذلك / فضلاً عن ذلك
← furthermore / moreover / in addition
علاوة على ذلكfurthermore / moreover
فضلاً عن ذلكfurthermore / in addition
بالإضافة إلى ذلكin addition to that
يُضاف إلى ذلكfurthermore / additionally
وزيادة على ذلكand moreover

8
however, since each animal species, in addition to what it shares with other animals, has specific attributes that single it out from the others. Through awareness of these attributes, knowledge becomes complete. Examples: defining man as “a mortal that speaks”.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
لأنّ لكلّ نوع من أنواع الحيوان بعد مشاركته لغيره في الحيوانية خاصّيّات يتميز بها من غيره، وبمعرفتها تتمّ المعرفة. مثل: أنّ حدّ الإنسان حي ناطق
9
In addition, it lacks all three requisites for qualifying as an intrinsic attribute, which are: being specific to one species only, being present throughout the entire species, and existing continually in the species.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
وليس له جميع شرائط الخاصة الثلاث، أعني الإختصاص بالنوع الواحد، والعموم لحميع النوع، ودوام الوجود في النوع.
10
Moreover, if it happens that the heat emanating from the heart and enveloping the whole body should be abnormal (ghariba), but is below the strength required to harm the natural functions of the body that is, those that exist in a healthy body it is not then termed fever.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
وإذا اجتمع أن تنبعث من القلب هى غريبة، فتشتمل البدن، إلأ أنها ليست من القوة بحيث تضرّ بالأفعال الطبيعية، أى الأفعال التي تصدر عن الحال الصحية، لم تسمَّ حمى.
+2 more pairs
علاوة على ذلك / فضلاً عن ذلك calques English "furthermore / moreover". Pre-modern Arabic used وأيضاً، وكذلك, and يُضاف إلى ذلك. These fixed additive connectors as standalone clause-introducers mirror "furthermore / moreover" as academic accumulation markers.
ظاهرة / ظواهر
← phenomenon / phenomena
هذه الظاهرةthis phenomenon
الظاهرة اللغويةthe linguistic phenomenon
ظاهرة اجتماعيةa social phenomenon
ظواهر متعددةmultiple phenomena
دراسة الظاهرةstudying the phenomenon
1
There is some licence here in describing “creation” and “decay” as movements. The reason is that, although the two phenomena represent change, they do not constitute true movements.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
وقد وقع التساهل في تسمىة الكون والفساد حركتين، لأنهما وإن كانا تغيرين، فليسا بحركتين
2
One may observe that everybody who recalls a past action of their own which they incite others to imitate says, “I always do such and such”; someone with a doctrine wishes that everybody would agree with him. This phenomenon can also be seen among the elements: when some become strong than others, they change them to their own substance: you can see how trees are formed, and how plants and trees are nourished by transforming water and the moisture in the soil to their own substance. For this may glory be given to Him who created and organized all things, the is no other God but He.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm
وترى كل من ذكر شيئًا يحض عليه يقول: وأنا أفعل أمرًا كذا. وكل ذي مذهب يود لو كان الناس موافقين له وترى ذلك في العنصر إذا قوي بعضها على بعض أحاله إلى نوعيته وترى ذلك في تركيب الشجر وفي تغذي النبات والشجر بالماء ورطوبة الأرض وإحالتهما ذلك إلى نوعيتهما فسبحان مخترع ذلك ومدبره لا إله إلا هو.
3
Every event (or phenomenon), whether (it comes into being in connection with some) essence or (as the result of an) action, must inevitably possess a nature peculiar to its essence as well as to the accidental conditions that may attach themselves to it. If the student knows the nature of events and the circumstances and requirements in the world of existence, it will help him to distinguish truth from untruth in investigating the historical information critically.muqaddimah
فإنّ كلّ حادث من الحوادث ذاتا كان أو فعلا لا بدّ له من طبيعة تخصّه في ذاته وفيما يعرض له من أحواله فإذا كان السّامع عارفا بطبائع الحوادث والأحوال في الوجود ومقتضياتها أعانه ذلك في تمحيص الخبر على تمييز الصّدق من الكذب
4
In connection with the protection of towns against harm that might arise from atmospheric phenomena, one should see to it that the air where the town is (to be situated) is good, in order to be safe from illness.muqaddimah
وممّا يراعى في ذلك للحماية من الآفات السّماويّة طيب الهواء للسّلامة من الأمراض.
5
No (phenomenon) of this sort one may hear about should be denied, but all these things should be understood to be the result of much civilization and the resulting great profits which facilitate spending and giving bounties to those who ask for them. This might be compared with the difference existing in one and the same town with regard to the houses dumb animals keep away from or frequent.muqaddimah
كلّ شيء يبلغك من مثل هذا فلا تنكره واعتبره بكثرة العمران وما يكون عنه من كثرة المكاسبة الّتي يسهل بسببها البذل والإيثار على مبتغيه ومثّله بشأن الحيوانات العجم مع بيوت المدينة الواحدة وكيف تختلف أحوالها في هجرانها أو غشيانها
6
In sum, the primary natural reason for this (situation) is the fact that dynasty and royal authority have the same relationship to civilization as form has to matter. (The form) is the shape that preserves the existence of (matter) through the (particular) kind (of phenomenon) it represents. It has been established in philosophy that the one cannot be separated from the other.muqaddimah
والسّبب الطّبيعيّ الأوّل في ذلك على الجملة أنّ الدّولة والملك للعمران بمثابة الصّورة للمادّة وهو الشّكل الحافظ بنوعه لوجودها. وقد تقرّر في علوم الحكمة أنّه لا يمكن انفكاك أحدهما عن الآخر.
7
Then, they considered (the fact) that the meaning changes with the change of vowel (endings). For this (phenomenon), they used the technical term of i'rab. For the thing that necessitates the change (in meaning), they used the technical term "agent," ('amil), and so on.muqaddimah
ثمّ رأوا تغيّر الدّلالة بتغيّر حركات هذه الكلمات فاصطلحوا على تسميته إعرابا وتسمية الموجب لذلك التّغيّر عاملا وأمثال ذلك.
8
So, too, for the Practice of God’s Emissary. Only a practice from God’s Emissary may abrogate another such practice. If God provides a new ruling to His Emissary concerning a matter for which His Emissary has already established a practice, one other than the practice established previously by God’s Emissary, then God’s Emissary would institute a new practice concerning the new ruling provided by God. This would clarify for people that the new practice of God’s Emissary abrogates the prior one to the extent it was inconsistent with the new one. This phenomenon is in fact discussed in accounts of his Practice (may God bless him and grant him peace).risala
وهكذا سنة رسول الله، لا يَنْسَخُها إلا سنةٌ لرسول الله؛ ولو أحدث الله لرسوله في أمر سنَّ فيه، غيَر ما سنَّ رسول الله: لَسَنَّ فيما أحدث الله إليه، حتى يُبَيِّنَ للناس أن له سنةً ناسخةَ لِلَّتي قّبْلَها مما يخالفها. وهذا مذكور في سنته - صلى الله عليه وسلم -.
ظاهرة calques English "phenomenon" as a named analytical object. Pre-modern kalām used ظاهرة only for outward physical accidents. The formula الظاهرة + [adj. noun] — الظاهرة الاجتماعية — mirrors "the [adj.] phenomenon" as an object requiring scholarly explanation.
منهج / أسلوب
← method / methodology
المنهج العلميthe scientific method
منهج البحثresearch methodology
المنهج المتبعthe method adopted
اعتمد المنهجadopted the method
أسلوب منهجيsystematic method
1
But these two methods of cauterization I do not consider right except on certain men; and I regard them as risky. In my opinion, to let the patient alone is preferable and safer, if it is to be. For the head is weakened when its natural continuity is broken, as we see in other parts, and particularly when the patient’s head is weak by nature.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
ولست أرى هذين النوعين من الكيّ البتة، إلا في بعض الناس وعلى طريق الغرر، وتركه عندي أفضل، ومع السلامة إذا كانت، فإن الرأس يضعف متى تفرق اتصاله الطبيعي، كما قد شاهدناه في سائر الأعضاء، ولا سيّما متى كان الرأس ضعيفاً بالطبع.
2
When an apoplexy has lasted a long while and has been treated by those methods we have given and your treatment has been ineffective but the patient has no fevert then bum him with four cauterizations: one on each frontal prominence; one in the middle of his head as we have described; and one at the back of the head as has been said.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
إذا أزمنت السكتة، وعالجتها بما ذكرنا ولم ينجع علاجك، ولم يكن بالعليل حمىّ، فاكوه أربع كيات؛ على كل قرن من رأسه كية، وكية في وسط الرأس كما ذكرنا، وكية في مؤخر الرأس على ما تقدم، وصفة المكاوي على ما تقدم.
3
We shall deal later on with the method of extraction and cutting out of arteries, and provision against haemorrhage.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وسنأتي بالحكمة في سَلّ الشريانات وقطعها والتحفظ من النزف.
4
When you have treated foetor by those methods given in its section and your treatment is unsuccessful, give the patient laxative draughts for three days; then shave his head; and bum with a median cauterization with the olivary cautery; then cauterize him twice above the eyebrows a little below the hair with a claviform cautery.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
إذا عالجته بما ذكرنا في التقسيم ولم ينجع العلاج، فبادر فاسق العليل القوقايا ثلاث ليال، ثم احلق رأس العليل، واكوه الكية الوسطى بالمكواة الزيتونية، ثم اكوه بالمكواة المسمارية كيتين فوق الحاجبين تحت الشعر قليلاً،
5
This is the method of burning with caustic: bid the patient let the lashes grow long and straight; then make the shape of a myrtle leaf out of paper.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وأما الكي بالدواء المحرق، فهو أن تأمر العليل أن يترك الأشفار حتى تطول وتستوي، ثم تصنع من الكاغد صورة ورقة آسة،
6
Another method of cauterizing a fistula is that which some of the Ancients mentioned: apply yourself to the site of the fistula and incise it; then insert into the incision a fine funnel, this being the figure of it (fig. 13),1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
ووجه آخر من كيّ الناصور ذكره بعض الأوائل؛ تعمد إلى موضع الناصور فتشقه ثم تضع في نفس الشق قمعاً رقيقاً هذه صورتها:
7
The second method is to heat the cautery provided with two prongs, described in the chapter on dislocation of the shoulder.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
والوجه الآخر في الكيّ، أ، تحمي المكواة ذات السفودين، التي ذكرنا في باب كيّ تخلّع المرفق،
8
Do not let the patient, after cauterization, be without the necessary treatment, so that you combine both methods for him, and a cure will speedily come to him.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
ولا تُخلِ العليل من العلاج بعد الكي بما ينبغي، ليجتمع فيه المعنيين، فيسرع إليه البرء إن شاء الله.
9
When the diarrhoea is due to frigidity and humidities so that the capacity of the stomach and intestines to hold and to digest is impaired, and this has been treated by various methods and is not cured;1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
إذا كان الإسهال من برد ورطوبات حتى أضعف القوى الماسكة والهاضمة التي في المعدة والأمعاء، وعولج ذلك بضروب العلاج ولم يبرأ،
10
The actual cautery will serve for several methods.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
فالكيّ بالنار يكون على وجوه كثيرة،
+2 more pairs
منهج as a named research methodology calques English "method/methodology". Pre-modern Arabic used طريقة (way/manner), أسلوب (style/approach), and مسلك (path/approach). The research-writing formula منهج البحث / المنهج العلمي mirrors English "research method / scientific method" as genre labels.
دليل / أدلة
← evidence / the evidence
الأدلة تشير إلىthe evidence suggests / points to
دليل علىevidence of / evidence for
أدلة كافيةsufficient evidence
بناءً على الأدلةbased on the evidence
الأدلة تدعمthe evidence supports
1
Then one should assess the seriousness of the illness which is determined by things known as “daughters of the foundations” (banat al-arkan, secondary considerations). These provide the evidence on which treatment is based, examples being the patient’s strength, age and temperament.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
ويتعرف مقدار العلة. وهذا يكون من الأشياء التي تسمى بنات الأركان، وهى الشواهد التي عليها مبني الأمر في العلاج، كقوة المريض، وسنّه، ومزاجه
2
by which I mean, the patient and the evidence on which “secondary considerations” are founded. Also, both schools employ one treatment per illness.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
أعني المريض والشواهد التي عليها مبني الأمر المسمى بنات الأركان، ويستعملان في المرض الواحد علاجًا واحدًا.
3
The difference between the two schools is that Empiricists diagnose a disease, find the evidence, and decide on a medication by drawing on memory (hifz) and observation (rasd). Dogmatists, on the other hand, arrive at the same end by deduction (istidlal).1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
والفرق بينهما: أن أصحاب التجربة يعرفون المرض والشّواهد وجميع ما تكون به المداواة "بالحفظ والرّصد". وأما أصحاب القياس فيعرفون هذا "بالاستدلال".
4
Otherwise, all people would regard themselves entitled to their own religious or scientific beliefs Because in their view they have evidence and proof to support their claims.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
لولا ذلك لكان الناس كلهم فيما ينتحلونه من الديانات والعلوم محقّين، لأنّهم يوردون على ما يعتقدونه، ويقولونه بالأدلة والبراهين.
5
Logic is also the gauge by which every analogical reasoning is measured, and the test by means of which the truth or falsehood of each piece of evidence is decided.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
والمنطق وهو المعيار الذي به يوزن كل قياس، والمحكّ الذي بتوسطه تعرف الصحة أو الفساد في كل دليل.
6
These are the major categories of pulse, and now we shall mention some of the sub-divisions appropriate for our book. One variety is the ghazali (like a gazelle), where the blood-vessel beats under the pulse-taker’s finger, then beats a second time, without giving evidence of its recurrence or pause.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
فهذه هى أجناس النّبض، ونحن نذكر من أنواعها ما يليق بكتابنا هذا. فمنها الغزالي، وهو أن يقرعك العِرق، ثم يقرعك قرعة ثانية من غير أن تحسّ برجوعه وسكونه
7
This it does by changing the quality of the air inside the body only, and the evidence of that is that the user feels only a slight change in his body.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
وذلك بأنْ يحلّ كيفية الهواءِ الذي في باطن البدن فقط. وعلامة ذلك أن يحسّ المستعمل له بتغيّر يسير ينال البدن.
8
He then said to them: ‘Suppose that I put Sofian to death in retaliation for the death of Ibn al-Mukaffa, and that Ibn al-Mukaffa himself then come forth from that door’ (pointing to one “which was behind him), ‘and speak to you; what should I do to you in that case? I should put you to death in retaliation for the death of Sofian.’ On this, the witnesses retracted their evidence, and Isa and Suleiman ceased to speak of their secretary, knowing that he had been killed with al-Mansur’s approbation. ibn al-Mukaffa lived (it is said) thirty-six years.”1282CE.wafayat-ibn-khallikan
ثم قال لهم: أرأيتم إن قتلت سفيان به ثم خرج ابن المقفع من هذا البيت - وأشار إلى باب خلفه - وخاطبكم ما تروني صانعاً بكم أأقتلكم بسفيان! فرجعوا كلهم عن الشهادة، وأضرب عيسى وسليمان عن ذكره، وعلموا أن قتله كان برضا المنصور. ويقال: إنه عاش ستاً وثلاثين سنة.
9
He then entered, and having given his evidence, the kadi said: “I have received your declaration and admitted your evidence.” {Being convinced, however, that they were false witnesses,) he paid the money out of his own purse and dismissed the Jew; but he did not dare to refuse Aba Dulama’s testimony through dread of his evil tongue. He thus quieted at the same time his fears and his conscience.1282CE.wafayat-ibn-khallikan
ثم حضرا بين يدي القاضي، وأديا الشهادة، فقال له: كلامك مسموع وشهادتك مقبولة، ثم غرم المبلغ من عنده وأطلق اليهودي، وما أمكنه أن يرد شهادتهما خوفاً من لسانه، فجمع بين المصلحتين وتحمل الغرم من ماله.
10
The shaikh Abu Ishak al-Shirazi mentions in his book, entitled al-Muhaddab, that Said Ibn Jubair could play at chess with his back turned, to the chess-board, and in the chapter on evidence, he cites this fact under the head of chess-playing, (it being questioned whether a chess-player's evidence could be received in a court of law).1282CE.wafayat-ibn-khallikan
وحكى الشيخ أبو إسحاق الشيرازي في كتاب "المهذب" أن سعيد بن جبير كان يلعب بالشطرنج استدباراً، ذكره في كتاب الشهادات في فصل اللعب بالشطرنج.
+2 more pairs
الأدلة تشير إلى / تدعم calques English "the evidence suggests / supports". Pre-modern Arabic used دليل، برهان, and حجة for proof, but always governed by a scholar, not as impersonal agents. The formula الأدلة تشير إلى — "the evidence suggests" — attributes agency to evidence as an independent analytical actor, mirroring English academic register.
مهم / بالغ الأهمية
← important / of great importance
أمر مهمan important matter
بالغ الأهميةof great importance / highly important
من المهم أنit is important that
أهمية كبيرةgreat importance
دور مهمan important role
1
When the wound is opened up a little with the instrument and the bowel is reduced, it is important to return it to its natural shape and proper position, if you can, for that is better.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
فإذا اتسع الجرح ودخل المعا فينبغي أن يكون رده على شكله الطبيعي ومكانه الخاص به إن استطعت على ذلك فهو أفضل.
2
But if you fear that the important organs may share the hurt of the wound, then you should dip some soft wool in moderately warm olive-oil or oil of roses and bind it round the various sites between the groin and the axillae.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
فإن خشيت أن يشارك الجرح الأعضاء الرئيسة في الألم، فينبغي أن تغمس صوفًا لينًا في الزيت المعتدل الحرارة، أو في دهن الورد، وتضعه حول المواضع التي فيما بين الأربية والإبط،
3
If there be nothing in the way like a blood-vessel or nerve or important organ, as we said.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
إن لم يمنعك مانع من عرق أو عصب أو عضو رئيس كما قلنا.
4
It is important also for the patient to take care to cleanse his body with laxative medicines from all corrupt and evil humours.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وينبغي أن يتعاهد العليل بتنقية جسمه بالأدوية المسهلة للأخلاط العفنة الردية.
5
Anyone who sees an important person too often regards him as less eminent and less important.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm
كثرة وقوع العين على الشخص يسهِّل أمره ويهوِّنه.
6
Mistrust itself is regarded by some as an absolute fault. This is not so, unless it leads the person who feels it to commit deeds not allowed by religion, or to adopt behaviour which is unsocial. In other cases mistrust can be steadfastness, and steadfastness is a virtue. As for the reproach made to me by ignorant adversaries who say that I put no value on anyone who disagrees with me when I believe that I am in the right, and that I would never act in concert with the ones I contradict even if they amounted to the entire human population on the face of the earth, and that I place no value on conforming with the people of my country in many of the customs or costume which they have adopted for no particular reason – this independence is a quality which I regard as one of my most important virtues. There is nothing equal to it, and, upon my life, if I did not possess it (God forbid), it would be this that I most longed for, and hoped for, and prayed for to God Almighty.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm
وأما سوء الظن فيعده قوم عيبًا على الإطلاق وليس كذلك إلا إذا أدى صاحبه إلى ما لا يحل وأما الذي يعيبني به جهال أعدائي من أني لا أبالي فيما أعتقده حقًا عن مخالفة من خالفته ولو أنهم جميع من على ظهر الأرض وإني لا أبالي موافقة أهل بلادي في كثير من زيهم الذي قد تعودوه لغير معنى فهذه الخصلة عندي من أكبر فضائلي التي لا مثيل لها ولعمري لو لم تكن في وأعوذ بالله لكانت من أعظم متمنياتي وطلباتي عند خالقي عز وجل.
7
There is no virtue which so much resembles a vice as the faculty of having many friends and acquaintances. But it is really a perfect virtue, made up of various qualities, since friends are only gained by tolerance, generosity, patience, loyalty, signs of affection, shared feelings, and moderation. It is important to protect one’s friends, teach them what one knows, and to win over them by every kind of praiseworthy action.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm
ليس شيء من الفضائل أشبه بالرذائل من الاستكثار من الإخوان والأصدقاء فإن ذلك فضيلة تامة متركبة لأنهم لا يكتسبون إلا بالحلم والجود والصبر والوفاء والاستضلاع والمشاركة والعفة وحسن الدفاع وتعليم العلم وبكل حالة محمودة.
8
Moreover, the knowledge that you are so proud of having penetrated is perhaps one of the less important branches of knowledge, of no great value, such as poetry or suchlike. You should then remember the man whose branch of study is more noble than yours on the scale of this world and the next, and your soul should become humble in your own sight.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm
ثم لعل علمك الذي تعجب بنفاذك فيه من العلوم المتأخرة التي لا كبير خصلة فيها كالشعر وما جرى مجراه فانظر حينئذ إلى من علمه أجل من علمك في مراتب الدنيا والآخرة فتهون نفسك عليك.
9
A proud man becomes proud because of an obvious merit: one man may be vain about his own scholarship, and be haughty and scornful towards others another may be vain about his man is proud of his own judgment and becomes arrogant; another is self-satisfied and becomes complacent. Another, full of his own reputation and high standing, becomes self-important and haughty.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm
فقد يكون العجب لفضيلة في المعجب ظاهرة فمن معجب بعلمه فيكفهر ويتعالى على الناس ومن معجب بعمله فيرتفع ومن معجب برأيه فيزهو على غيره ومن معجب بنسبه فيتيه ومن معجب بجاهه وعلو حاله فيتكبر ويتنخى.
10
And likewise I have seen a man bearing an injustice speak calmly without complaining and only displaying a little anxiety. At first glance, without further examination, you would have taken him for the oppressor. In cases such as this it is important to establish the facts, fight resolutely against our inclination to take sides, not incline towards or against such attitudes as we have described, and seek to be impartial to all, as we are obliged to by justice.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm
ورأيت بعض المظلومين ساكن الكلام معدوم التشكي مظهرًا لقلة المبالاة فيسبق إلى نفس من لا يحقق النظر أنه ظالم وهذا مكان ينبغي التثبت فيه ومغالبة ميل النفس جملة وإن لا يميل المرء مع الصفة التي ذكرنا ولا عليها ولكن يقصد الإنصاف بما يوجبه الحق على السواء.
+2 more pairs
بالغ الأهمية / من المهم أن calques English "of great importance / it is important that". Pre-modern Arabic used عظيم الشأن (of great affair), خطير (serious/weighty), and جليل (sublime). The academic genre formula من المهم أن نُلاحظ — "it is important to note" — mirrors English conventions.
مقارنة / يقارن
← compare / comparison / in comparison with
مقارنةً بـcompared to / in comparison with
الدراسة المقارنةcomparative study
بالمقارنة معin comparison with
قياساً علىby comparison with / analogously to
عند المقارنةupon comparison
1
But he who holds the opposite view says that actual cautery may help in the case of such hot dry illnesses as arise in the human body; for when you compare the human body, humid as it is, with the nature of fire you find the human body cold. I myself from much experience agree with this last opinion.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وقال الذي يقول بضد ذلك إن الكيّ بالنار قد ينتفع به في مرض حارَّ يابس يحدث في أبدان الناس، لأنك متى أضفت بدن الإنسان ورطوبته إلى مزاج النار، أصبت بدن الإنسان بارداً وأنا أقول بقوله، لأن التجربة قد كشفت لي ذلك مرات،
2
But if it be due to an escape of bowel the change of position, compared with what we have described, will be greater, and it will disappear on digital pressure, and then will recur; and often there will be crepitus with it, and it will increase in size on entering the bath or on violent exertion.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وإن كان من قبل خروج المعا، فيكون وضعه على ما وصفنا، أشد اختلافًا، فإذا كبسته بأصابعك يغيب ثم يرجع، وربما كان معه قرقرة، ويعظم كثيرًا عند دخول الحمام والتعب الشديد،
3
A dislocation down into the axilla may be diagnosed by comparing the sound and the dislocated humerus; for you will find an obvious difference between them; you will find a hollow at the head of the humerus, and in the axilla the head of the humerus may be palpated like an egg, and the patient is unable to lift his hand to his ear, and full movements are restricted.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وتعرف الفك إذا كان الى أسفل نحو الإبط بأن تقرن بين المنكب المفكوك والمنكب الصحيح، فإنك تجد بينهما خلافًا ظاهرًا، وتجد رأس المنكب فيه تقعير، وتحت الإبط عند اللمس رأس المنكب كأنه بيضة، ولا يقدر العليل أن يرفع يده الى أذنه، ولا أن يحركها جميع الحركات،
4
Whatever way it is dislocated it will not be concealed from you, because it is both visible and palpable, and whenever you compare the dislocated elbow with the sound one it is quite openly and plainly manifested; for the elbow joint is hollow and the patient cannot flex the forearm back or touch his shoulder with it.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وفكه لا يخفى عليك، لأنه واقع تحت البصر، وتحت اللمس على أي شكل انفك، فإذا قرِنت المِرفق المفكوك بالصحيح يتبين لك بيانًا ظاهرًا لتقعر المفصل، ولا يستطيع أن يثني الذراع، ولا يمس به منكبه.
5
The indication of an internal dislocation is that when you compare the patient's sound leg with the injured leg it will be Longer and the knee will be prominent as compared with that of the sound leg; nor will it be possible for the patient to flex the foot back on the groin; and there will be an obvious swelling near the groin because the head of the femur has moved there.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وعلامة فكه إلى داخل، أنك إذا قرنت ساق العليل الصحيحة بالمريضة تكون أطول، وتكون الركبة ناتئة أكثر من الصحيحة، ولا يقدر أن يثني رجله عند الأربية، ويكون الموضع الذي يلي الأربية وارمًا، من قِبَل أن رأس الفخذ قد صار إلى هناك.
6
Then loosen the bandage and the plaster, and compare the leg with the other.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
ثم حل الرباط والضماد، وقس الساق بالأخرى،
7
then compared such observations to his own experiences and derived through reasoning similar or comparable conclusions; and then others followed and acquired his knowledge, interpreted it and enlarged upon it.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
ثم قاس على ما ثبت من ذلك في نفسه، واستخراج النظائر والمشاكلات بفكره، ثم تلاه من الناس من يتلقَّن علمَه ويضيف إليه ويزيد فيه
8
using observation and comparison as he had done when all this happened, the medical profession came into being and was firmly established among those concerned and interested in the subject.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
بأن يرصد مثل ما يرصد ويقيس مثل ما قاس، أفضى الأمر إلى حصول صناعة الطب وتقوّمها في نفوس المتعاطين لها، والمهتمين بها
9
and to deduce distinctive characteristics on the basis of their experience. They compared their findings with basics already established through observation and by seeing with the naked eye. It is through this process that the medical profession developed in India, Persia and Byzantium.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
وإلى استخراج الخاصيَّات بالتجربة، وإلى القياس على الأصول الحاصلة بالرصد والمشاهدة. فقامت الصناعة الطبية بهذا التدبير في الهند والفرس والروم،
10
Fields of knowledge depending on the senses are few and insignificant compared to the rational sciences.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
والعلوم الحسيّة في غاية القلّة والنّزارة إذا أضيفت إلى العلوم العقلية.
+2 more pairs
المقارنة / الدراسة المقارنة calques English "comparison / comparative study". Pre-modern Arabic used القياس (analogy/measure). The systematic foregrounding of comparison as a named scholarly operation — الدراسة المقارنة — is a modern genre convention.
يتجاوز / يتخطى
← go beyond / goes beyond
يتجاوزgoes beyond / transcends
يتخطى حدودgoes beyond the limits of
يتجاوز نطاقgoes beyond the scope of
تجاوز المألوفgoes beyond the conventional
1
Indeed, if we were to go beyond elements and composite structures, we find that the Sun, the Moon and all other planets affect these elements, as well as the composite structures made from these elements.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
ثم إذا تجاوزنا هذه العناصر والمركبات منها، وجدنا الشمس والقمر وسائر الكواكب تؤثر في هذه العناصر والمركبات منها.
2
This example demonstrates the extent to which the advocates of analogy (the Dogmatists) can be innovative. Empiricists, on the other hand, are unable to use the above reasoning, because they do not go beyond what they observe through their senses.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
فعلى هذا المثال يتسع المجال على "أصحاب القياس" جدًّا. فأما "أصحاب التجربة"، فلا يمكنهم ذلك، لأنّهم لا يتجاوزون حواسّهم
3
The second degree is ascribed to things that have a greater evident effect on the body, for their influence goes beyond changing the air in the body and begins to change its moisture.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
والدرجة الثانية: هى الأشياء التي تؤثر في البدن تاثيرًا أبين من ذلك، بأن تتجاوز إحالة الهواء إلى إحالة رطوبة الجسد.
4
The definition of advice is that the man giving it feels bad about what harms his friend, whether the latter feels good or bad about it, and he feels happy about what is good for him, whether his friend is happy or unhappy about it. This is the added factor which a counsellor has which goes beyond the limit of simple friendship.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm
وحد النصيحة هو أن يسوء المرء ما ضر الآخر ساء ذلك الآخر أو لم يسؤه وإن يسره ما نفعه سر الآخر أو ساءه فهذا شرط في النصيحة زائد على شروط الصداقة.
5
On this some person said to him: “Commander of the faithful! why not cut it off by the wrist” “God forbid!” exclaimed the caliph; how could he then lean on his staff? How could he pray? How “could he eat?” When al-Hajjaj Ibn Yusuf arrived at Basra, Ali Ibn Asma went to him and said: “O emir! my parents treated me most foully in naming me Ali; give me another name.” “You come,” replied al-Hajjaj, “with an excellent pretext to excite my interest; I appoint you director of the fisheries at al-Baraja with a daily salary of two danaks in copper-money; but, by Allah! if you go beyond that sum, I shall cut off the portion of your hand which Ali left on.”1282CE.wafayat-ibn-khallikan
فقيل له: يا أمير المؤمنين ألا قطعته من زنده، فقال: يا سبحان الله، كيف يتوكأ كيف يصلي كيف يأكل فلما قدم الحجاج بن يوسف البصرة أتاه علي بن أصمع فقال: أيها الأمير، إن أبوي عقاني فسمياني علياً، فسمني أنت، فقال: ما أحسن ما توسلت به، قد وليتك سمك البارجاه، وأجريت لك في كل يوم دانقين فلوساً، ووالله لئن تعديتهما لأقطعن ما أبقاه علي من يدك.
6
O women! you who condemn the fondness shown for the abode of a person dearly beloved, (for a spot which is so) difficult to reach by the random efforts even of our wishes, leave the lover to [his attachment for the land of) Najd, the sole occupation of his heart. Were even Najd a valley, that heart would never go beyond it. Suppose you hinder him from seeing the beloved with his eyes, can you hinder his heart from longing to possess her?.1282CE.wafayat-ibn-khallikan
ألومه في حب دار عزيزة ... يشق على رجم المطامع مرماها\r\nدعوه ونجداً إنها شأن قلبه... فلو أن نجداً تلعة ما تعداها\r\nوهبكم منعتم أن يراها بعينه... فهل تمنعون القلب أن يتمناها
7
Moses did not become weary until he went beyond where God had commanded him.923CE.Tarikh.Tabari
قَالَ: وَلَمْ يَجِدْ موسى النَّصَبَ حَتَّى جَاوَزَ حَيْثُ أَمَرَهُ اللَّهُ،
8
If any of them deviated from this, or distinguished himself and went beyond this limitation by advancing well beyond his province, then it was due rather to his personality and not entrenched rule, or ancestry and progeny.923CE.Tarikh.Tabari
فإن نزع منهم نازع، أو نبغ منهم نابغ فتجاوز ذلك- وإن بعدت مسافة سيره من مخلافه- فإنما ذلك منه عن غير ملك له موطد، ولا لآبائه، ولا لأبنائه،
9
He ordered his ministers and provincial governors not to go beyond what was just in the course of their official duties and not to act in any way during the course of those duties except justly.923CE.Tarikh.Tabari
فأمر وزراءه وأصحاب أعماله ألا يتعدوا فيما هم بسبيله العدل، ولا يعملوا في شيء منه إلا به،
10
In this manner, people acquire perceptive and apperceptive knowledge, which is the product of thinking in the body. All this is (the result of the power of) imagination and limited in extent, since from the way it starts it can reach the primary (intelligibilia) but cannot go beyond them. Also, if they are corrupt, everything beyond them is also corrupt.276 This, as a rule, is the extent of human corporeal perception. It is the goal of the perceptions of scholars. It is in it that scholars are firmly grounded.muqaddimah
يستفيدون به العلوم التّصوّريّة والتّصديقيّة الّتي للفكر في البدن وكلّها خياليّ منحصر نطاقه إذ هو من جهة مبدئه ينتهي إلى الأوّليّات ولا يتجاوزها وإن فسد فسد ما بعدها وهذا هو في الأغلب نطاق الإدراك البشريّ الجسمانيّ وإليه تنتهي مدارك العلماء وفيه ترسخ أقدامهم.
+2 more pairs
يتجاوز calques English "go beyond / transcend" in the abstract scholarly sense. The formula X يتجاوز Y إلى Z — "X goes beyond Y to Z" — as a claim of analytical scope extension mirrors English scholarly register.
مفهوم
← concept / notion / the concept of
مفهوم Xthe concept of X
مفهوم مركزيa central concept
المفاهيم الأساسيةkey concepts
إطار مفاهيميconceptual framework
مفهوم نظريa theoretical concept
1
Nor let enter your minds, my sons, the groundless notion of the laity and of ignorant doctors, that the complaint cured by the cautery never comes back later; do not cleave to this opinion.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
ولا يقع ببالكم يا بَنِيَّ ما يتوهّمه العامة وجهال الأطباء، أن الكي الذي يُبرئ من مرض ما، لا لذلك المرض عودة أبداً، ويجعلونه لزاماً،
2
The term [iskul] which is Syriac, comprises two concepts: one is “understanding and learning”, and the other is “the place of learning”.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
وهذه اللفظة اسم سرياني مركّب من شيئين: أحدهما: الفهم والتفهم، والثاني: الموضع الذي فيه يتفهم ما يتفهم.
3
for geometry as wise men have rightly said opens a door in the mind equal to one thousand other openings. Through it, a student gets a clear notion of analogy.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
فإن الهندسة كما قال الحكماء، وحقًا قالوا، تفتح عين العقل الذي الواحدة من عيونه خير من ألف عين من العيون التي في الرأس، وتصور للمتعلم صورة القياس.
4
That is why each scientist and craftsman who made a new discovery had to invent terms to coincide with the new concepts he had discovered or the things he had devised.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
ولذلك احتاج كل من مستنبطي العلوم والصناعات أن يخترع ألفاظًا يطابق بها المعاني التي استنبطها، والأشياء التي استخرجها.
5
and without the terms that they had invented for the concepts they had discovered and promulgated. Since medicine shares so much with philosophy that it is essentially a part of it.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
والألفاظ التي اخترعوها للمعاني التي عثروا عليها وأثاروها. ولمّا كان الطب يشارك الفلسفة حتى أنه جزء من أجزائها
6
if we suppose that the notion of being an animal is non-existent, then the human being ceases to exist, while if it is assumed a human being exists then the quality of being an animal exists.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
ومتى فرض المعنى الذي هو الحيوانية معدومًا، عدم الإنسان. ومتى فرض الإنسان موجودًا، وجب وجود الحيوانية.
7
The name (al-ism): This term denotes the general concept of a thing, such as when we say “a human being”, which is a term that leaves us with the image of a human being, but does not furnish details.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
الاسم: وهو اللفظ الدال على ذات الشيء مفصلة من الأشياء مجملًا، كقولنا: "الإنسان"، فإنّه لفظة يقع منه في نفوسنا صورة الإنسان، إلا أنه لا يفصل لنا معناه.
8
These general terms are five in number because the general concepts are five in number. These are: genus, variety, species, the particular and the accidental.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
وهذه الألفاظ الكلية، خمسة بالعدد. لأنّ المعاني الكلية أيضًا خمسة: وهى الجنس والفصل والنوع والخاصة والعرض.
9
An example of an accidental quality is “white” hair: one can imagine hair existing whether it has this quality or not. Know that the universal concept represented by these five terms is known as “genus”.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
كالبياض الذي يمكن أن تتصور الشعرة موجودة مع عدمه عنها. واعلم أن المعاني الكلية التي تدل عليها هذه الألفاظ الخمسة يسمى "جنسًا"
10
that the concept “the one who speaks” is known as a “variety”, that the concept referred to by “human being” is called a “species”.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
ومعنى قوله "الناطق" يسمى فصلًا، والمعنى الذي يدل عليه قولنا "الإنسان" يسمى نوعًا
+2 more pairs
مفهوم calques English "concept". Pre-modern Arabic used المعنى (meaning), المقصود (the intended sense), and الاصطلاح (technical term). The formula مفهوم X — "the concept of X" — as a named theoretical object, and إطار مفاهيمي (conceptual framework), mirror English scholarly register.
في الواقع / في حقيقة الأمر
← in fact / as a matter of fact
في الواقعin fact / in reality
في حقيقة الأمرas a matter of fact
والحقيقة أنّthe truth is that
في واقع الأمرin actual fact
1
If you wish, you may cauterize it down to the bone with an edged cautery so that the ends of the artery be divided; for this will replace that operation. It is in fact better, as we have said, unless the patient is feverish or of a hot constitution; cauterization is one of the means of eliminating humidities, so it will be of surer efficacy.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وإن شئت أن تكويه كيًا إلى العظم بمكواة سكينية، حتى تنبتر أطرفه، فيقوم مقام هذا العمل بعينه أو أفضل كما قلنا، إلا إن كان العليل به حمى، أو كان محرور المزاج، لأن الكي مما يعين على إفناء الرطوبات، فيكون أوكد في المنفعة إن شاء الله تعالى.
2
In fact I thought that there was no more disease in the bone beyond what I had cut and sawn away, and that I had completely eradicated it.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وأنا أظن أن ليس في العظم غير ذلك الفساد الذي قطعته ونشرته وأني قد استأصلته.
3
It is in fact a small solitary worm generated between the skin and the flesh, that creeps all over the body, both up and down.1013CE.at-tasrif-zahrawi
وهي دودة صغيرة واحدة تتولد بين الجلد واللحم، وتدب في الجسم كله صاعدًا وهابطًا.
4
This group claims that they use both experience and analogy. In fact, they are far removed from both; they make their claim without any proof that it reflects reality.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
وهم يزعمون أنهم يستعملون التجربة والقياس جميعًا، وما بينهم وبينها أبعد البعد، وإنما هو قول يخرج من أفواههم من غير أنّ يبين على صفحات أحوالهم.
5
then mixes them together and produces the humours, and only then does it fashion the faculties and the organs. Our method of teaching should in fact be the opposite to nature’s order of creation.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
ثم تمزجها فتحصل منها الأخلاط، ثم تفعل القوى والأعضاء. فيجب أن يكون طريقنا في التعليم بالعكس من طريقة الطبيعة في التكوين.
6
It might be said, however, that this substance is the cause of the damage to the eye because it produces the ophthalmia. In fact, it is nearer to the truth to say that the cause of damage is the ophthalmia. As for the flowing disease matter, it is harmful through the mediation of the ophthalmia.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
قد يقال أنه يضر بفعل العين، من جهة أنه هو الذي يولّد الرمد. لكن المضرّ القريب بالحقيقة هو الرمد. فأمّا المادة المنصبّة، فإنمّا أضرّت بتوسّط الرمد.
7
nor one which is hot in the second, third or fourth degree and, at the same time, moist in the second, third or fourth degree. The effects we have mentioned are the main properties of food and medicaments which, in fact, have other secondary effects.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
ولا دواء حارّ في الدرجة الثانية أو الثالثة أو الرابعة رطب في الدرجة الثانية أو الثالثة أو الرابعة. والقوى التي ذكرناها هي القوى الأُوَل للغذاءِ والدواءِ، ولهما قوى ثوان
8
There are some who by nature dislike sensual pleasures and scorn those who seek after them, such as those men we have just mentioned, and who prefer to lose a fortune rather than gain one. Some prefer ignorance to knowledge, in fact most of the people that you see in the street are like this.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm
وفي الناس من يبغض اللذات بطبعه ويستنقص طالبها كمن ذكرنا من المؤثرين فقد المال على اقتنائه وفي الناس من يؤثر الجهل على العلم كأكثر من ترى من العامة.
9
These words encapsulate all virtue: to control oneself against passion means in fact to turn away from one’s natural tendency towards anger and lust, things which are both under the dictates of passion. Then all that is left for the soul to use is the intellect which God has given it, the good sense which distinguishes it from the beasts, from insects or vermin and from tigers.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm
جامع لكل فضيلة لأن نهي النفس عن الهوى هو ردعها عن الطبع الغضبي وعن الطبع الشهواني لأن كليهما واقع تحت موجب الهوى فلم يبق إلا استعمال النفس للنطق الموضوع فيها الذي به بانت عن البهائم والحشرات والسباع.

Do not put off to tomorrow what you can do today. If you recognize this obligation you will make haste to do today even very small preparations for tomorrow, for if a small number of tasks are left to mount up they become a great number. In fact they have become too many to do and the whole enterprise will be wrecked.1046CE.Al-Akhlaq.Ibn.Hazm

في الواقع / في حقيقة الأمر calques English "in fact". Pre-modern Arabic used في الحقيقة، بيد أن, and بل for epistemic reorientation. The modern formula في الواقع as a systematic clause-introducing discourse marker mirrors English "in fact".

الغرض من / الهدف من calques English "the aim/purpose of" as a nominalised research-purpose announcement. Pre-modern Arabic used غرضه (his purpose), قصده (his aim), مراده (his intention). The formula الغرض من هذا الكتاب / هدف الدراسة as a standing nominal phrase mirrors English genre conventions of announcing study purpose.
يُؤدّي دوراً مهماً / يقوم بدور
← plays a key role / plays an important role
يُؤدّي دوراً مهماًplays an important role
يقوم بدور رئيسيplays a key/leading role
يلعب دوراً محورياًplays a central/pivotal role
يضطلع بدورplays a role / takes on a role
دوره محوريits role is central/pivotal
1
Definition plays a major role in teaching wisdom, because a concise statement such as “a mortal speaking being” denotes a single individual of the Homo sapiens species, which is countless and limitless in number.1031CE.miftah-al-tibb
فكانت للحد المنفعة العظيمة في تعليم الحكمة، لأن القول الوجيز، مثل (الحي الناطق المائت)، يوقف به على أشخاص نوع الإنسان التي لا تحصى ولا تُحصر.
يُؤدّي دوراً مهماً / يلعب دوراً محورياً calques English "plays a key/important role". Pre-modern Arabic expressed the same through يقوم مقام (stands in the place of), كان شأنه عظيماً (its matter was weighty), or له وزن (it carries weight). The collocational frame دور + evaluative adjective mirrors the English pattern "[adj.] role" as an analytical formula.

هذه الدراسة / هذه الورقة calques English "this paper / this study / the present study". Pre-modern Arabic used هذا الكتاب (this book), هذه المقالة (this treatise), هذا الباب (this chapter). The contemporary academic formula هذه الدراسة تهدف إلى / تسعى هذه الورقة إلى — "this study aims to / this paper seeks to" — mirrors English genre conventions of the study/paper as a named agent with explicit aims.

2 Al-ShawkinI says in the Nay I al-An>ldr. “The scholars have disagreed about the land that was conquered after the mobilization of the armies (janwwatan).'" Ibn al-Mundhir says that al-Shafi(I maintained that HJmar pleased those who had the right to spoils and had annexed the Sawad lands. He said that the hukrn (rule) for land conquered after fighting is that it be distributed as spoils just like the Prophet (pbuh) distributed the land of Khaybar. The jurists disagreed about the land that <'Umar retained without distribution. The majority of the jurists maintained that he treated it as a trust through the representatives of the Muslims and instituted khardj for it and also prohibited its sale. Some of the jurists of Kufa said that he let it remain in the ownership of those non-bclicvcrs who were settled on it and imposed khardj on them. يُقرّر أنّ / يصرّح بأنّ calques English "maintains that / asserts that". Pre-modern Arabic used قال (said), ذكر (mentioned), and زعم (claimed). The academic formula يُقرّر الباحث أنّ X / يصرّح بأنّ X — "the researcher maintains / asserts that X" — as a nuanced attribution of scholarly claims mirrors English academic register.

خلاصة القول / وجملة ذلك calques English "in conclusion / to sum up". Pre-modern Arabic used وجملة ذلك (the sum of that), وحاصل الأمر (the upshot), and وفي الجملة (in general). من الضروري / ينبغي أن calques English "it is necessary / it is essential". Pre-modern Arabic used ينبغي (one should), يجب (it is obligatory), and لازم (necessary). The academic genre formula من الضروري أن نُشير — "it is necessary to note" — as a self-referential methodological statement mirrors English "it is necessary to / it is essential to" conventions.
